Block 3 compiles. Now we need the fundamental theorem and the final theorem. Let me write Block 4: environment relation, fundamental theorem, and the final proof.

Environment relation:
```
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T ->
    E T (sigma1 x) (sigma2 x) /\ locally_closed (sigma1 x) /\ locally_closed (sigma2 x).
```

Fundamental theorem:
```
Lemma fundamental : forall (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty),
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma1 sigma2, env_rel Gamma sigma1 sigma2 ->
  E T (subst_env sigma1 t) (subst_env sigma2 t).
```

Proof by induction on the typing derivation with the as-pattern, similar to has_type_lc. For each rule:

T_Var: goal E T (subst_env σ1 (fvar x)) (subst_env σ2 (fvar x)) = E T (σ1 x) (σ2 x). Hypothesis Hlook : lookup_context x Gamma = Some T. Apply env_rel with x T Hlook → H : E T (σ1 x) (σ2 x) ∧ lc ∧ lc. exact (proj1 H). Note the env_rel hypothesis name: in the induction, the goal has the form `forall sigma1 sigma2, env_rel Gamma σ1 σ2 -> E T ...`; we should intro sigma1 sigma2 Henv.

T_Unit: goal E (Ty_Unit k) (unit k) (unit k). unfold E; intros v1 v2 He1 He2. Inversion He1: E_Unit gives v1 = unit k; He2 similarly. subst. apply V_unit_iff; split; [ann_reflexive? | split; [..| erasure]].

Need: ann k (tm_unit k): security_le (top_security (unit k)) k = security_le k k → security_le_refl. Erase equality: reflexivity.
So:
```
  - unfold E. intros v1 v2 He1 He2. inversion He1; subst. inversion He2; subst.
    apply V_unit_iff. split; [unfold ann; apply security_le_refl | split; [unfold ann; apply security_le_refl | reflexivity]].
```
Careful: inversion He1 on eval (tm_unit k) v1: E_Unit only; v1 = tm_unit k. Good. Then He2 similar.

T_Abs: term tm_abs T1 body kappa, type Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa. Goal E (arrow) (abs T1 (subst_env σ1 body) kappa) (abs ... σ2 ...).
unfold E; intros v1 v2 He1 He2. Inversion He1: E_Abs: v1 = tm_abs T1 (subst σ1 body) kappa AND value (that abs); He2 similarly. subst.

      The commutation lemma needs (forall y, lc_at 0 (σ y)). Our env_rel currently only gives lc for y in Gamma's domain. For y not in the domain, σ y arises only if the term has a free variable y in Gamma — since typing gives fv t ⊆ Gamma's domain, the free variables of terms are in Gamma. Hmm, the body's fvars: since the T_Abs premise is typed under update Gamma x T1, its free vars are in Gamma ∪ {x}. And the body's fvars are among those (opening adds x but the body's fvars are preserved/increased by x). So all fvars of body are in Gamma ∪ {x}. Thus we only need lc for σ y where y ∈ Gamma's domain or y = x. The commutation lemma, though, requires ALL y. We can strengthen env_rel to require (forall y, lc_at 0 (σ y)) globally, plus the E-relatedness for y in Gamma. Since we choose σ to be identity elsewhere (id_env), which is lc. So define:
```
Definition env_rel Gamma σ1 σ2 :=
  (forall y, lc_at 0 (σ1 y)) /\ (forall y, lc_at 0 (σ2 y)) /\
  forall x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> E T (σ1 x) (σ2 x).
```
This is stronger and easier for the commutation. And when extending, we need all values lc: arg1 is a value so lc; σ's global lc gives the rest. Good. And the initial env for the final theorem (extend x t1 id_env) has global lc: t1 is lc_at 0 (from has_type_lc) and id_env is lc. 

Let me redefine env_rel this way:
```
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall y, lc_at 0 (sigma1 y)) /\
  (forall y, lc_at 0 (sigma2 y)) /\
  (forall x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> E T (sigma1 x) (sigma2 x)).
```

Lemma env_rel_extend : forall Gamma σ1 σ2 x u1 u2 T1,
  env_rel Gamma σ1 σ2 -> value u1 -> value u2 -> V T1 u1 u2 ->
  env_rel (update Gamma x T1) (extend x u1 σ1) (extend x u2 σ2).

  intros Gamma σ1 σ2 x u1 u2 T1 [Hlc1 [Hlc2 Hrel]] Hv1 Hv2 HV.
  split; [| split].

  - apply subst_env_extend_lc; [exact Hlc2 | apply value_lc; exact Hv2].

    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst y.

        -- apply Nat.eqb_eq in E2; subst y; contradiction.
        -- apply Hlc1.
      * unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E2.
        -- apply Nat.eqb_eq in E2; subst y; contradiction.
        -- apply Hlc2.
Qed.
```

Actually simpler: use a lemma `extend_neq : forall x y u σ, y <> x -> extend x u σ y = σ y`. Define:
```
Lemma extend_neq : forall (x y : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm), y <> x -> extend x u sigma y = sigma y.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E; auto. apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. contradiction. Qed.
Lemma extend_same : forall (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm), extend x u sigma x = u.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.
```

V_E_value:
```
Lemma V_E_value : forall (T : ty) (v1 v2 : tm),
  value v1 -> value v2 -> V T v1 v2 -> E T v1 v2.
Proof.
  intros T v1 v2 Hv1 Hv2 HV. unfold E. intros w1 w2 He1 He2.
  assert (w1 = v1) by (apply eval_value_unique; auto). subst w1.
  assert (w2 = v2) by (apply eval_value_unique; auto). subst w2.
  exact HV.
Qed.
```

Let me redo V with term matching for unit/sum/prod. For unit, no need to match (erase equality). For sum and prod, match v1 and v2.

Definition:
```
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (v1 v2 : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit k =>
      ann k v1 /\ ann k v2 /\
      match k.ind with Low => erase_security v1 = erase_security v2 | High => True end
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 k =>
      match k.ind with
      | Low =>
          ann k v1 /\ ann k v2 /\
          match v1, v2 with
          | tm_inl a1 ka1, tm_inl a2 ka2 => V T1 a1 a2
          | tm_inr a1 ka1, tm_inr a2 ka2 => V T2 a1 a2
          | _, _ => False
          end
      | High => True
      end
  | Ty_Prod T1 T2 k =>
      match k.ind with
      | Low =>
          ann k v1 /\ ann k v2 /\
          match v1, v2 with
          | tm_pair a1 b1 ka1, tm_pair a2 b2 ka2 => V T1 a1 a2 /\ V T2 b1 b2
          | _, _ => False
          end
      | High => True
      end
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 k =>
      match k.ind with
      | Low => ann k v1 /\ ann k v2 /\
          forall r u1 u2, lc u1 -> lc u2 -> E T1 u1 u2 -> E T2 (app v1 u1 r) (app v2 u2 r)
      | High => True
      end
  end.
```

OK so the plan: V with term matching for sum/prod (Low cases), ann ∧ ann retained. Let me rewrite V:

```
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (v1 v2 : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit k =>
      ann k v1 /\ ann k v2 /\
      match k.(indirect_reader) with
      | Low => erase_security v1 = erase_security v2
      | High => True
      end
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 k =>
      ann k v1 /\ ann k v2 /\
      match k.(indirect_reader) with
      | Low =>
          match v1, v2 with
          | tm_inl a1 _, tm_inl a2 _ => V T1 a1 a2
          | tm_inr a1 _, tm_inr a2 _ => V T2 a1 a2
          | _, _ => False
          end
      | High => True
      end
  | Ty_Prod T1 T2 k =>
      ann k v1 /\ ann k v2 /\
      match k.(indirect_reader) with
      | Low =>
          match v1, v2 with
          | tm_pair a1 b1 _, tm_pair a2 b2 _ => V T1 a1 a2 /\ V T2 b1 b2
          | _, _ => False
          end
      | High => True
      end
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 k =>
      ann k v1 /\ ann k v2 /\
      match k.(indirect_reader) with
      | Low => forall r u1 u2, locally_closed u1 -> locally_closed u2 ->
                 E T1 u1 u2 -> E T2 (tm_app v1 u1 r) (tm_app v2 u2 r)
      | High => True
      end
  end.
```

Hmm, the guard checker: V calls itself on T1/T2 in the sum/prod matches; those are subterms, fine even under the term matches (the recursion argument doesn't change). Coq's guard checker might complain because the recursive call is inside a match on v1/v2? No, the principal argument is T; matching on other arguments is fine.

```
Lemma eval_abs_inv : forall (T : ty) (body : tm) (k : security) (w : tm),
  eval (tm_abs T body k) w -> w = tm_abs T body k /\ value (tm_abs T body k).

Lemma eval_pair_inv : forall (t1 t2 : tm) (k : security) (w : tm),
  eval (tm_pair t1 t2 k) w ->
  exists v1 v2, w = tm_pair v1 v2 k /\ eval t1 v1 /\ eval t2 v2.
Proof. intros. inversion H; subst; eauto. Qed.

Lemma eval_inl_inv : forall (t : tm) (k : security) (w : tm),
  eval (tm_inl t k) w -> exists v, w = tm_inl v k /\ eval t v.
Proof. intros. inversion H; subst; eauto. Qed.

Lemma eval_inr_inv : ...
Lemma eval_app_inv : ...

Lemma eval_protect_inv : forall l t w, eval (tm_protect l t) w ->
  exists v, w = protect_value v l /\ eval t v.

Lemma eval_if_inv : ...

  apply V_sum_structural in HV; [| exact Hi].
  destruct HV as [[w1 [w2 [k1 [k2 [E1 [E2 H1]]]]]] | [w1 [w2 [k1 [k2 [E1 [E2 H1]]]]]]].
  - injection E1 as -> ->. exists w2, k2. split; [exact E2 | exact H1].
  - exfalso. rewrite E1 in *. discriminate.
```
Hmm, in the second case E1 : tm_inl a1 ka1 = tm_inr w1 k1; `discriminate E1` directly. Let me write `discriminate E1`.
Wait, in the second branch the exists is `exists w1 w2 k1 k2, v1 = tm_inr w1 k1 /\ v2 = tm_inr w2 k2 /\ V T2 ...`; E1 : v1 = tm_inr w1 k1 where v1 = tm_inl a1 ka1. So E1 : tm_inl a1 ka1 = tm_inr w1 k1. discriminate.

V_sum_inv_inr similar.

Let me carefully write the proof.

```coq
Lemma fundamental : forall (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty),
    has_type Gamma t T ->
    forall (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> tm),
      env_rel Gamma sigma1 sigma2 ->
      E T (subst_env sigma1 t) (subst_env sigma2 t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T HT.
  induction HT as
    [ Gamma0 x T0 Hlook Hwf
    | Gamma0 k Hwf
    | L Gamma0 T1 body T2 kappa Hwf1 Hwf2 Hprem IHprem
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 H2 IH2 Hflows
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa H1 IH1 H2 IH2 Hwf
    | Gamma0 t T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 Hflows
    | Gamma0 t T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 Hflows
    | Gamma0 t T1 T2 kappa H1 IH1 Hwf2 Hwfsec
    | Gamma0 t T1 T2 kappa Hwf1 H1 IH1 Hwfsec
    | L Gamma0 t b1 b2 T1 T2 T0 kappa r H1 IH1 Hflows Hprem1 IHprem1 Hprem2 IHprem2
    | Gamma0 t T0 l H1 IH1
    | Gamma0 t T0 U H1 IH1 Hsub
    | Gamma0 c yes no kappa T0 r H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3 Hflows ];
    intros sigma1 sigma2 Henv.
```
Hmm, the `; intros sigma1 sigma2 Henv` applies to all 13 goals. Good.

Let me check the direction convention: In Coq, `destruct H as ->` where H : a = b rewrites the goal replacing a by b (left-to-right). With `injection H as -> ->`, the equations from injection are oriented as they appear: tm_inl va2 ka2 = tm_inl va2' ka2' gives va2 = va2' and ka2 = ka2'. So `->` replaces va2 by va2' — va2 disappears. To keep va2, use `<-` (replaces va2' by va2).

Let me replace `injection Eq as -> ->.` with `injection Eq as <- <-.` in T_Case (both occurrences) and T_If. Also check other injection uses:
- T_Abs: `injection Ef1 as -> -> ->.` where Ef1 : tm_abs Ta ba ka = tm_abs T1 (subst σ1 body) {rk,Low}. `->` replaces Ta by T1, ba by subst σ1 body, ka by {rk,Low}. That's what we want (the subsequent Hres1 refers to ka/ba which get replaced; good).
- T_Fst: `injection Eq as -> -> ->.` where Eq : tm_pair a2 b2 k2 = tm_pair a2' b2' k2'. `->` replaces a2 by a2', b2 by b2', k2 by k2'. The subsequent `exact HV1` where HV1 : V T1 a1 a2' — goal V T1 a1 a2 (after w substitutions). After replacing a2 by a2' in the goal, the goal becomes V T1 a1 a2'. Good.
- T_Snd similar.

For T_Case, using `<-` keeps va2/ka2 and replaces va2'/ka2' by them. Then HVp : V T1 va1 va2' becomes V T1 va1 va2. Good.

This is the same as subst_plug after rewriting with subst_env_extend_id. So I need both.

Let me define ctx_atoms and prove the substitution-through-context lemma.

```
Fixpoint ctx_atoms (C : program_context) : list atom :=
  match C with
  | C_Hole => []
  | C_Abs T C' k => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_AppLeft C' t2 r => ctx_atoms C' ++ fv t2
  | C_AppRight t1 C' r => fv t1 ++ ctx_atoms C'
  | C_PairLeft C' t2 k => ctx_atoms C' ++ fv t2
  | C_PairRight t1 C' k => fv t1 ++ ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Fst C' r => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Snd C' r => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Inl C' k => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Inr C' k => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_CaseScrutinee C' b1 b2 r => ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b1 ++ fv b2
  | C_CaseLeft t0 C' b2 r => fv t0 ++ ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b2
  | C_CaseRight t0 b1 C' r => fv t0 ++ fv b1 ++ ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Protect l C' => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_IfCondition C' y n r => ctx_atoms C' ++ fv y ++ fv n
  | C_IfThen c C' n r => fv c ++ ctx_atoms C' ++ fv n
  | C_IfElse c y C' r => fv c ++ fv y ++ ctx_atoms C'
  end.
```
Then:
```
Lemma subst_plug : forall (C : program_context) (x : atom) (u : tm),
  ~ In x (ctx_atoms C) -> subst x u (plug C (tm_fvar x)) = plug C u.
Proof.
  intros C. induction C; simpl; intros x u Hx.
```
Each case: the subst distributes; use subst_fresh for the fixed terms (requiring ~In x (fv t)), and the IH for the sub-context (requiring ~In x (ctx_atoms C')).

Let me write it:
- C_Hole: subst x u (fvar x) = if x=?x then u else fvar x = u. `simpl. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.` Wait: plug C_Hole (fvar x) = fvar x; subst x u (fvar x) = if Nat.eqb x x then u else fvar x = u. Hx : ~ In x [] = trivial. So `simpl. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.` But `simpl` might reduce Nat.eqb x x? Not automatically; use rewrite.
- C_Abs T C' k: plug = abs T (plug C' (fvar x)) k; subst = abs T (subst x u (plug C' (fvar x))) k; IH with Hx : ~In x (ctx_atoms C') gives = abs T (plug C' u) k = plug (C_Abs T C' k) u. So `simpl. rewrite IHC. reflexivity.` with IHC applied: `rewrite (IHC x u Hx)`.
- C_AppLeft C' t2 r: ctx_atoms = ctx_atoms C' ++ fv t2. Hx gives ~In x (ctx_atoms C') and ~In x (fv t2) via not_in_app. Then `simpl. rewrite (IHC x u H1), (subst_fresh x u t2 H2). reflexivity.`
- C_AppRight t1 C' r: ctx_atoms = fv t1 ++ ctx_atoms C'; not_in_app gives both. simpl; rewrite subst_fresh, IHC.
- C_PairLeft/Right similar.
- C_Fst/Snd/Inl/Inr: ctx_atoms = ctx_atoms C'; simpl; rewrite IHC.
- C_CaseScrutinee C' b1 b2 r: ctx_atoms = ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b1 ++ fv b2 = ctx_atoms C' ++ (fv b1 ++ fv b2). Need three. Use not_in_app twice.
- C_CaseLeft t0 C' b2 r: fv t0 ++ ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b2 = fv t0 ++ (ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b2). Split.
- C_CaseRight: fv t0 ++ fv b1 ++ ctx_atoms C' = fv t0 ++ (fv b1 ++ ctx_atoms C'). Split.
- C_Protect: ctx_atoms C'; simpl; rewrite IHC.
- C_IfCondition: like CaseScrutinee.
- C_IfThen: fv c ++ ctx_atoms C' ++ fv n.
- C_IfElse: fv c ++ fv y ++ ctx_atoms C'.

Note: In each case, after `simpl`, the goal is an equality of terms with subst applied inside; rewriting with the IH should work if the occurrences match. The IH: forall x u, ~In x (ctx_atoms C') -> subst x u (plug C' (fvar x)) = plug C' u. Rewriting `rewrite (IHC x u H1)` should replace subst x u (plug C' (fvar x)) with plug C' u. And subst_fresh x u t2 H2 : subst x u t2 = t2. Good.

But careful: `simpl` on `subst x u (plug (C_AppLeft C' t2 r) (fvar x))` — plug unfolds to tm_app (plug C' (fvar x)) t2 r; subst unfolds to tm_app (subst x u (plug C' (fvar x))) (subst x u t2) r. Good.

Inductive multi : tm -> tm -> Prop :=

Theorem noninterference : forall (C : program_context) (t1 t2 : tm)
    (T_secret T_result : ty),
  has_type empty t1 T_secret ->
  has_type empty t2 T_secret ->
  context_has_type C T_secret T_result ->
  ground T_result ->
  transparent T_result ->
  ~ flows_to (security_of T_secret).(indirect_reader)
      (security_of T_result).(indirect_reader) ->
  same_result (plug C t1) (plug C t2).

```
Lemma env_rel_single : forall (x : atom) (T_hole : ty) (t1 t2 : tm),
  locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t2 ->
  E T_hole t1 t2 ->
  env_rel (update empty x T_hole) (extend x t1 id_env) (extend x t2 id_env).
Proof.
  intros x T_hole t1 t2 Hlc1 Hlc2 HE. split.
  - intros y. unfold extend, id_env. destruct (Nat.eqb x y); auto.
  - split.
    + intros y. unfold extend, id_env. destruct (Nat.eqb x y); auto.
    + intros y U Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E.
      * apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst y.
        assert (HU : U = T_hole).
        { rewrite lookup_context_update_same in Hy. injection Hy as Hinv. symmetry. exact Hinv. }
        subst U. rewrite extend_same, extend_same. exact HE.
      * apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
        rewrite (lookup_context_update_neq empty y x T_hole E) in Hy. simpl in Hy. discriminate Hy.
Qed.
```

Qed.

program_context constructors:
C_Hole
C_Abs : ty -> program_context -> security -> program_context

C_AppRight : tm -> program_context -> label -> program_context

ctx_atoms:
```
Fixpoint ctx_atoms (C : program_context) : list atom :=
  match C with
  | C_Hole => []
  | C_Abs T C' k => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_AppLeft C' t2 r => ctx_atoms C' ++ fv t2
  | C_AppRight t1 C' r => fv t1 ++ ctx_atoms C'
  | C_PairLeft C' t2 k => ctx_atoms C' ++ fv t2
  | C_PairRight t1 C' k => fv t1 ++ ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Fst C' r => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Snd C' r => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Inl C' k => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Inr C' k => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_CaseScrutinee C' b1 b2 r => ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b1 ++ fv b2
  | C_CaseLeft t0 C' b2 r => fv t0 ++ ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b2
  | C_CaseRight t0 b1 C' r => fv t0 ++ fv b1 ++ ctx_atoms C'
  | C_Protect l C' => ctx_atoms C'
  | C_IfCondition C' y n r => ctx_atoms C' ++ fv y ++ fv n
  | C_IfThen c C' n r => fv c ++ ctx_atoms C' ++ fv n
  | C_IfElse c y C' r => fv c ++ fv y ++ ctx_atoms C'
  end.
```
Note ++ is right-assoc, so `ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b1 ++ fv b2` = ctx_atoms C' ++ (fv b1 ++ fv b2). And `fv t0 ++ ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b2` = fv t0 ++ (ctx_atoms C' ++ fv b2). Fine.

subst_plug by induction on C:
The induction principle for program_context: each constructor with recursive C' argument (one recursive occurrence, for those constructors that have one). The IHs: one per recursive context. As-patterns:

program_context_ind will have for each constructor a premise; the recursive occurrence gets an IH. Let me just use `induction C; simpl; intros x u Hx` and then handle with bullets, using auto-generated names (IH C etc.). To avoid naming issues, I'll use as-patterns:
- C_Hole: []
- C_Abs: [T C' k IH']
- C_AppLeft: [C' t2 r IH']
- C_AppRight: [t1 C' r IH']
- C_PairLeft: [C' t2 k IH']
- C_PairRight: [t1 C' k IH']
- C_Fst: [C' r IH']
- C_Snd: [C' r IH']
- C_Inl: [C' k IH']
- C_Inr: [C' k IH']
- C_CaseScrutinee: [C' b1 b2 r IH']
- C_CaseLeft: [t0 C' b2 r IH']
- C_CaseRight: [t0 b1 C' r IH']
- C_Protect: [l C' IH']
- C_IfCondition: [C' y n r IH']
- C_IfThen: [c C' n r IH']
- C_IfElse: [c y C' r IH']

Based on the tm_ind pattern, each recursive argument is immediately followed by its IH in the argument order of the constructor. For C_Abs: args ty, program_context, security; the recursive is the 2nd; the intro order is T, C', IH, k. So the as-pattern should be [T C' IH k]. Similarly:
- C_AppLeft: args C', tm, label → [C' IH t2 r]

- C_PairLeft: [C' IH t2 k]

- C_CaseRight: args tm, tm, C', label → [t0 b1 C' IH r]

- C_IfThen: [c C' IH n r]
- C_IfElse: [c y C' IH r]

- C_Hole: goal subst x u (fvar x) = u; simpl; rewrite Nat.eqb_refl; reflexivity. Hx unused.

The supplied statement:
```
Theorem noninterference : forall (C : program_context) (t1 t2 : tm)
    (T_secret T_result : ty),
  has_type empty t1 T_secret ->
  has_type empty t2 T_secret ->
  context_has_type C T_secret T_result ->
  ground T_result ->
  transparent T_result ->
  ~ flows_to (security_of T_secret).(indirect_reader)
      (security_of T_result).(indirect_reader) ->
  same_result (plug C t1) (plug C t2).
```

Proof:
```
Proof.
  intros C t1 t2 T_secret T_result Ht1 Ht2 Hctx Hg Htr Hflow.
  destruct Hctx as [L Hctx].
  destruct (flows_to_inv _ _ Hflow) as [Hsec_ind Hres_ind].
  assert (Hsec : security_of T_secret = {| reader := High; indirect_reader := High |}).
  { apply wf_security_high_eq.
    - apply wf_ty_security. apply has_type_wf. exact Ht1.
    - exact Hsec_ind. }
  destruct (exists_fresh_two L (ctx_atoms C)) as [x [HL Hxc]].
  assert (Henv : env_rel (update empty x T_secret)
                   (extend x t1 id_env) (extend x t2 id_env)).
  { apply env_rel_single.
    - apply has_type_lc in Ht1. exact Ht1.
    - apply has_type_lc in Ht2. exact Ht2.
    - unfold E. intros v1 v2 He1 He2. apply V_high. exact Hsec. }
  pose proof (fundamental (update empty x T_secret) (plug C (tm_fvar x)) T_result
                (Hctx x HL) (extend x t1 id_env) (extend x t2 id_env) Henv) as HE.
  assert (Hsub1 : subst_env (extend x t1 id_env) (plug C (tm_fvar x)) = plug C t1).
  { rewrite subst_env_extend_id. apply subst_plug. exact Hxc. }
  assert (Hsub2 : subst_env (extend x t2 id_env) (plug C (tm_fvar x)) = plug C t2).
  { rewrite subst_env_extend_id. apply subst_plug. exact Hxc. }
  rewrite Hsub1, Hsub2 in HE.
  intros v1 v2 Hev1 Hev2.
  destruct Hev1 as [Hm1 Hval1]. destruct Hev2 as [Hm2 Hval2].
  apply multi_eval in Hm1; [| exact Hval1].
  apply multi_eval in Hm2; [| exact Hval2].
  unfold E in HE.
  pose proof (HE v1 v2 Hm1 Hm2) as HV.
  apply (V_erase T_result v1 v2 Hg).
  - apply (transparent_all_ind_low (security_of T_result) T_result Htr Hres_ind).
  - exact HV.
Qed.
```

Let me write a brief summary of the approach for the user. Also consider saving a memory? The instructions about memory: this is a one-off task; probably not needed. But there's a persistent memory directory; the task is a proof benchmark. I don't think a memory is warranted unless something non-obvious and durable about the user's preferences. Skip.

Let me summarize the proof architecture concisely:
1. Added a big-step evaluation relation `eval` equivalent (for our purposes) to multi-step evaluation, with inversion lemmas.
2. Logical relation: `V T v1 v2` on values, guarded by the type's indirect_reader:
   - If the type's indirect reader is High, the relation is True.
   - Unit: erasure equality when Low.
   - Sum/Prod: matching constructors with component relations (Low); also `ann` records the value's top security is ≤ the type's security (needed to know runtime protections are Low).
   - Arrow: application relation.
   `E T t1 t2` = all pairs of evaluations yield V-related values.
3. Proved fundamental theorem by induction on typing with related environments (instantaneous substitution `subst_env`), using locally nameless lemmas (open/subst commutation, lc facts).
4. Subtyping monotonicity of the relation (V_sub/E_sub), protection lemmas (E_protect).
5. Final: derive secret.ind = High and result.ind = Low from the flows_to assumption; V at the secret type is universal; apply fundamental theorem to the plugged context (choosing a fresh hole variable not occurring in the context, so substitution through the context is the identity on fixed parts); convert multi-step evaluations to big-step; use `transparent`+`ground` to push indirect-reader Low to all subcomponents, and V_erase gives erased equality.

I'll report completion with the key facts. No need to include enormous detail.
